This is the ColorWay Sports MLB daily uniform tracker: what every team actually wore, every game, every day, logged the morning after. Not just the City Connects and throwbacks, the whole wardrobe, home whites and road grays included, because you can't spot the patterns without the full record. Every matchup also gets a ColorWay grade for how the two uniforms looked sharing a field.

Wondering what jersey your team is wearing tonight? Our [MLB uniform schedule guide](/stories/mlb-uniform-schedule-2026) and the per-team schedule pages linked at the bottom of this tracker map what all 30 teams plan to wear and when, so you can call tonight's uniform before first pitch. This page is the receipts: what they actually put on.

## Friday, July 24

Fifteen games and one of the most fun uniform nights of the season, because this was [Hall of Fame Weekend](/stories/mlb-throwback-caps-hall-of-fame-weekend-2026). All 30 clubs wore throwback caps to mark the induction ceremony, and the jerseys followed suit with a City Connect and alternate blowout: the Rays' Devil Rays fauxback, Boston's green CC, the Rangers' red Tejas set, Baltimore's cream-and-green BMORE, Philadelphia's blue Philly, and plenty more. Only one game on the entire slate had both teams in their standard uniforms, and there is a reason for that, which we get into below. The Angels' throwback “Los Angeles” caps take Jersey of the Day, the clean Dodgers-Mets primary matchup is our Matchup of the Day, and the slate grades out to 8 out of 10.

<div style="margin: 2em 0 0.6em; padding: 0.7em 1.1em; background: linear-gradient(135deg, #BA0021 0%, #1c1c1c 100%); border-radius: 12px 12px 0 0;">
  <span style="font-size: 0.72em; font-weight: 800; letter-spacing: 0.16em; text-transform: uppercase; color: #f5f5f5;">⚾ Jersey of the Day</span>
  <span style="display: block; font-size: 1.15em; font-weight: 900; color: #ffffff; margin-top: 3px;">Angels Throwback “Los Angeles” Caps</span>
</div>

<blockquote class="twitter-tweet" data-dnt="true"><a href="https://twitter.com/Angels/status/2080808306034274741"></a></blockquote>

On a night when all 30 teams wore throwback caps for Hall of Fame Weekend, the Angels' was our favorite. Los Angeles paired the road gray with a vintage “Los Angeles” cap, a clean nod to the franchise's California Angels era, and it was the sharpest of the throwback lids across the league. It is a simple change, just a cap, but it turned a plain road-gray look into the best-dressed detail of the day.

The one game on the board where both teams wore their standard uniforms, and it is a beauty for it. Los Angeles in the classic road gray with the script Dodgers, New York in the clean home white with the blue and orange: no City Connect, no alternate, just two of the sport's most timeless looks sharing a field. There is a fun reason it was the only primary-on-primary matchup of the night, too, with a [Hollywood movie shooting scenes at Citi Field](/stories/mets-dodgers-standard-uniforms-emma-stone-movie) this week, and sometimes the simplest frame is the best one.

San Francisco's orange alternate with the black Giants script is one of the boldest solid-color tops in the National League, and it glows against the Angels' road gray. Orange-on-gray is a bright, unmistakable San Francisco look, but the Halos take the night's spotlight with their throwback caps.

## Thursday, July 23

A five-game Thursday getaway slate, and it played about as quiet as that number suggests. Four of the five home teams reached for a white or a classic, the road clubs did most of the talking in their alternates, and nothing on the board came close to the color a full Friday delivers. Atlanta's home white was the sharpest thing in a dull room and takes Jersey of the Day, the Rays and Blue Jays get Matchup of the Day almost by default, and San Diego's brown ran for a seventh straight game. The slate grades out to 6.5 out of 10.

<div style="margin: 2em 0 0.6em; padding: 0.7em 1.1em; background: linear-gradient(135deg, #13274F 0%, #1c1c1c 100%); border-radius: 12px 12px 0 0;">

  <span style="display: block; font-size: 1.15em; font-weight: 900; color: #ffffff; margin-top: 3px;">Braves Home White</span>
</div>

<blockquote class="twitter-tweet" data-dnt="true"><a href="https://twitter.com/Braves/status/2080364443897119142"></a></blockquote>

On a night this short on color, the cleanest classic wins by default, and Atlanta's home white is a very good classic. The lowercase Braves script across the chest, the tomahawk underline, and the navy-and-red trim on a crisp white is one of the more timeless looks in the National League, and it does not need a gimmick to carry a broadcast. It is not the flashiest jersey on any given night, but on a quiet Thursday it was the sharpest thing on the field.

Tampa Bay stayed in the deep navy with the sunburst RAYS wordmark, and instead of answering in another blue, Toronto went to the home whites with the split BLUE JAYS script and the maple-leaf bird on the chest. Navy against clean white and blue is a more traditional frame than the all-blue nights, and after three of those in a week it was the change of pace the series needed.

The quiet end of a quiet night. Kansas City went with the road gray and the basics, no powder blue in sight, and Detroit stayed in the home whites with the Old English D. Gray against white is the plainest frame the sport offers and there was nothing here to change that, but the Tigers' clean home look kept it from bottoming out.

## Wednesday, July 22

Seventeen games, the biggest slate this tracker has logged in a single day, and we have Tuesday's rain to thank for it. Both games washed out at Yankee Stadium and Fenway came back as doubleheaders, and both twin bills ran the identical wardrobe script: road gray in the afternoon, black alternate under the lights. Twelve alternates hit the field in all and not one City Connect, which is exactly what a Wednesday is supposed to look like. The color that did show up was worth the wait, with the Rays and Blue Jays running their third blue-on-blue in four days for our Matchup of the Day, Kansas City finally reaching for the powder blue, Detroit's navy earning Jersey of the Day, and San Diego's brown hitting a sixth straight game. The slate grades out to 7.3 out of 10.

<div style="margin: 2em 0 0.6em; padding: 0.7em 1.1em; background: linear-gradient(135deg, #0C2340 0%, #1c1c1c 100%); border-radius: 12px 12px 0 0;">

  <span style="display: block; font-size: 1.15em; font-weight: 900; color: #ffffff; margin-top: 3px;">Tigers Navy Alternate</span>
</div>

<blockquote class="twitter-tweet" data-dnt="true"><a href="https://twitter.com/Tigers/status/2080125012674056230"></a></blockquote>

Detroit's navy alternate is the quiet success story of the 2026 uniform year. The orange Detroit script sitting on navy gives the Tigers something their gray road set never has, which is warmth, and it manages to look brand new while borrowing nothing but the team's own colors. It travels well too. Against Wrigley Field's pinstripes it read cleanly all afternoon, and Hao-Yu Lee's three-run homer in it was the highlight of Detroit's day. This is how you add an alternate without wandering away from who you are.

Three blue-on-blue games in four days between these two, and this is the best-looking one yet because they finally swapped ends of the spectrum. Tuesday it was Tampa Bay in the light blue against Toronto in the royal. Wednesday the Rays went to the deep navy with the sunburst RAYS wordmark and the Blue Jays answered in the powder blue, so instead of two mid-tone blues competing for the same space you get the darkest jersey either club owns against the lightest. That is how you do a single-color matchup: pick opposite ends and let the contrast carry it. No white and no gray anywhere in the frame for the second straight meeting, and the Rogers Centre roof kept the light even enough that both blues read true on camera.

Detroit's navy alternate is the best thing to happen to that uniform set in years, and it keeps proving it on the road. The orange Detroit script on navy is warmer and more legible than anything the block-D grays give you, and against Wrigley's white pinstripes with the red C it makes a proper old-school National League frame out of an interleague game. Two clubs with genuinely classic identities, neither one hiding behind a gimmick.

Tuesday's rainout turned into a Wednesday twin bill, and the opener went traditional. Pittsburgh's road gray carries more than most: the gold Pittsburgh script with the black outline is one of the few gray chests in the sport with real color on it, and the black-and-gold cap does the rest. Against the Yankee pinstripes and the interlocking NY it is about as close to a 1970s postcard as the modern schedule allows.

The nightcap got the black, and the black is the move. Gold on black under the Bronx lights against white pinstripes is a genuinely strong frame, high contrast without either club leaving its own palette. Worth flagging for the pattern watchers: we have been tracking a theory that Pittsburgh saves the black alternate for Paul Skenes starts, and this one puts a dent in it. Skenes did not pitch either end of the doubleheader, Bubba Chandler started the nightcap, and the black came out anyway. The jersey looks like a night-game call more than a pitcher call.

Baltimore's black with the orange Orioles script is the sharper of the two black tops that showed up on Wednesday, mostly because orange on black is a warmer contrast than gold on black, and the orange-brimmed black cap ties it together properly. Against Boston's home whites it gives Fenway a night look with some actual weight to it.

Third straight day of this exact pairing in Seattle, and it grades where it has graded all series. Cincinnati's gray carries the red CINCINNATI across the chest with the wishbone C on the cap, and Seattle's home whites lean on the navy and northwest green with the compass logo. Two well-built classic sets under a bright afternoon at T-Mobile Park, no surprises in either direction.

Miami's road gray is the most improved gray in baseball since the rebrand, with the black-outlined Miami script and the blue and red trim giving it more life than most, and Houston's home whites with the navy ASTROS wordmark and the orange piping are as clean as that franchise has looked in a decade. Two good uniforms, zero risk taken, and the sixth 6.5 on a slate that ran long on whites and grays.

## Tuesday, July 21

Rain took two games off the board, with the Pirates at Yankees and the Orioles at Red Sox both postponed, and the 13 that survived split hard between the clubs that dressed up and the clubs that clocked in. Eight teams went out in plain road gray and eight in home whites, but the color that did show up was excellent: the Rays and Blue Jays ran blue on blue for our Matchup of the Day, the Twins and Guardians both reached for alternates, San Diego's brown hit a fifth straight day, and the Dodgers went back to the royal blue. The slate grades out to 7.4 out of 10.

<div style="margin: 2em 0 0.6em; padding: 0.7em 1.1em; background: linear-gradient(135deg, #005A9C 0%, #1c1c1c 100%); border-radius: 12px 12px 0 0;">
  <span style="font-size: 0.72em; font-weight: 800; letter-spacing: 0.16em; text-transform: uppercase; color: #f5f5f5;">⚾ Jersey of the Day</span>
  <span style="display: block; font-size: 1.15em; font-weight: 900; color: #ffffff; margin-top: 3px;">Dodgers Royal Blue Alternate</span>
</div>

<blockquote class="twitter-tweet" data-dnt="true"><a href="https://twitter.com/Dodgers/status/2079759189518225542"></a></blockquote>

The Dodgers have been leaning on the royal blue alternate more and more, and it is the correct instinct. The blue with the white Los Angeles script across the chest is a better jersey than either gray they own, it photographs cleanly under the lights, and it gives a team that spends most of its road schedule in muted gray an actual identity on the field. Max Muncy put a two-run homer into the night in it at Citizens Bank Park. More of this, please.

The Jersey Matchup of the Day, and it is the second blue-on-blue meeting between these two in three days. Tampa Bay's columbia blue with the sunburst is the best jersey the Rays own, and Toronto answered again with the royal blue alternate, the arched BLUE JAYS wordmark over the bird crest. Two blues this far apart in value should not work together, and they do, because pale sky against deep royal reads as contrast rather than clash. Nothing else on this board put two colors on the field with no white and no gray anywhere in the frame.

Minnesota went to the navy alternate with the Twins script, Cleveland to the big red top with the block GUARDIANS wordmark, and the result is the sharpest color pairing the AL Central has produced in weeks. Red and navy is the oldest contrast in baseball for a reason, and when both teams reach for their alternates on the same night the broadcast gets better for free.

The royal alternate with the white Los Angeles script is a better-looking jersey than either of their grays, and against Philadelphia's red pinstripes it makes one of the best-dressed matchups the National League can produce. Bright blue against white-and-red pinstripes is a real color story rather than two teams in laundry.

Five home runs between them and both teams in their plainest clothes. Seattle wore the home whites with the navy MARINERS script and the teal underline, Cincinnati the road gray with the red CINCINNATI wordmark. These are two well-drawn, well-behaved uniforms that were never going to make anybody look twice.

## Monday, July 20

Monday nights are usually when the wardrobe goes quiet, and this 15-game slate mostly proved it: nine teams in plain road gray, eight in home whites or pinstripes, and exactly one City Connect on the entire board. The clubs that did put color on the field carried the night. The White Sox and Rangers gave us black against the cleanest whites in baseball for our Matchup of the Day, the Rays and Blue Jays went blue on blue, the Mets' royal alternate met Milwaukee's home cream, Houston broke out the white Space City City Connect, and the Angels wore the white throwback alternate for a second straight day. The slate grades out to 7.3 out of 10.

<div style="margin: 2em 0 0.6em; padding: 0.7em 1.1em; background: linear-gradient(135deg, #BA0021 0%, #1c1c1c 100%); border-radius: 12px 12px 0 0;">
  <span style="font-size: 0.72em; font-weight: 800; letter-spacing: 0.16em; text-transform: uppercase; color: #f5f5f5;">⚾ Jersey of the Day</span>
  <span style="display: block; font-size: 1.15em; font-weight: 900; color: #ffffff; margin-top: 3px;">Angels White Throwback Alternate</span>
</div>

<blockquote class="twitter-tweet" data-dnt="true"><a href="https://twitter.com/Angels/status/2079433697749819557"></a></blockquote>

Back-to-back Jersey of the Day honors for the same uniform, and it earned them again. The Angels' white throwback alternate, arched red "Angels" script, halo floating over the A, California patch on the sleeve, walked off the Cardinals on a hit by pitch and looked better doing it than anything else on the Monday board. This is the jersey the Angels should be wearing every home game.

The Jersey Matchup of the Day, and it is a study in how far clean beats busy. Chicago's black alternate with the gothic SOX wordmark is the sharpest thing in a wardrobe that has not had much to celebrate, and Texas answered with the whitest whites in the league, that unbroken cream-free home set with the royal and red piping running down the placket. Black against pure white is the oldest contrast in the book, and under the Globe Life roof it reads like a tuxedo. No City Connect, no throwback, no gimmick, just two teams wearing their best plain clothes.

A walk-off bunt deserved better clothes. San Francisco brought the road gray with the orange SF-orange trim and Kansas City wore the home whites with the powder-blue-adjacent royal script, saving the actual powder blues for another day. The Royals' home white is a genuinely underrated jersey, that curly script in royal with gold accents, but pair anything with plain gray and the whole frame settles down.

Pittsburgh's plain black alternate with the gold script is the jersey they trust, and putting it against the Yankee Stadium pinstripes is one of the better looks the schedule can produce. Black and gold against navy-on-white pinstripes is high contrast without being loud, two of the most conservative wardrobes in baseball both dressed up. The Pirates keep quietly wearing one of the sport's best black jerseys and nobody talks about it.

Blue on blue, and it worked. Tampa Bay's columbia blue alternate with the sunburst is the best jersey the Rays own, and Toronto answered with the royal blue alternate, the arched BLUE JAYS wordmark and the bird crest. Two different blues in one frame is a risk and this one lands because the values are so far apart, pale sky against deep royal. Color-on-color games are still the easiest way to make a Monday look like an event.

Los Angeles wore the regular road gray with the royal Dodgers script and the red number, not the alternate gray, and Philadelphia went with the home pinstripes and the red Phillies script. Gray at pinstripes is not a showstopper, but these are two of the five best scripts in baseball facing each other, and the Phillies' red-on-white-with-red-pinstripes is a genuinely beautiful uniform.

The only City Connect on the entire Monday board, and it is one of the good ones. Houston wore the white Space City jersey, the navy ASTROS wordmark cut through by the big orange-to-yellow gradient star, the nebula-print sleeve trim, and the "we choose to go to the moon" line stitched down by the hem. Miami answered with the plain black alternate, script Marlins in teal and red, not the Retro Wave City Connect. Black against a white City Connect gives the Astros' star and sleeve gradients all the room they need, and it is the rare Monday game where somebody actually dressed up.

Colorado's purple alternate is the only true purple jersey in the league, and it does a lot of work against a plain road gray. Washington's gray with the arched WASHINGTON in navy and red is a well-built road set, and purple against gray at altitude is a better-looking frame than the standings suggest. Nobody else can wear this color, which is exactly why the Rockies should wear it more.

The white throwback alternate with the arched red Angels script, the halo over the A, and the California sleeve patch is a second straight day of work for a uniform that deserves it, and St. Louis brought the road gray with the birds on the bat and that dark navy helmet. Retro white against classic gray is a warm, old-fashioned frame.

Detroit wore the road gray with the orange-trimmed Detroit script, Chicago the home pinstripes with the blue-and-red wishbone C. ## Sunday, July 19

Sunday in baseball means throwback and alternate jerseys, and this 16-game slate delivered the goods: the Mariners debuted the black-and-cream Steelheads throwback in a walk-off win, the Tigers' brand-new navy alternate met the Angels' white throwback alternate in our Matchup of the Day, Paul Skenes pitched in the Pirates' black alternate right on schedule, and the White Sox and Blue Jays went full color-on-color. The Sunday slate grades out to 7.3 out of 10.

St. Louis followed up the Victory Blue show with the road grays, but here is the detail that saves it: the dark navy batting helmet over the gray jersey, one of the best helmet-and-uniform combinations in baseball. The birds-on-the-bat over gray with that deep helmet shell is quietly elite, and it nudges an otherwise plain gray-on-white pairing up a half point.

## Saturday, July 18

Saturday flipped the wardrobe from Friday's City Connect night to a loaded alternate jersey showcase: 15 games, a Cleveland doubleheader, and some of the best uniform pairings of the week. The Cardinals' Victory Blue jersey steals Jersey of the Day, the Braves' light blue City Connect and the Red Sox's yellow Boston marathon jersey kept the color coming, the Athletics broke out the gold "Sacramento" script, and a Mets-Phillies rivalry classic in road gray and cream takes the Matchup of the Day crown. The Saturday slate grades out to 7.2 out of 10.

The powder-toned jersey with the birds-on-the-bat chest is a top-five alternate uniform in baseball for us, a look that manages to feel both vintage and fresh, and it wears even better under the lights. Straight from the Cardinals' own feed, on the field and not on the rack.

## Friday, July 17

Friday means City Connect night across baseball, and a full 14-game slate delivered. We logged everything from the marquee Dodgers-Yankees classics crossover to a Fenway doubleheader and a parade of City Connect jerseys: the Marlins' black "MIAMI" night look, Milwaukee's "Wisco," the Rockies' blue-and-purple gradient, and the Royals' white crown. It is a jersey-and-uniform lover's Friday, and the slate grades out to a solid 7.5 out of 10.

It is a clean, moody Pacific Northwest look that stands out on a night full of louder City Connects, proof that a great alternate does not need neon to win the day. Each day we spotlight one team's look straight from their own feed, so you see the jersey and uniform on the field and not just on the rack.

## Thursday, July 16

Baseball is back. The All-Star break wrapped and the second half opened with a single marquee game, the Mets at the Phillies in Philadelphia. New York went with the road grays and Philadelphia broke out the powder blue, and one game was all we needed to settle the style matchup. On a one-game night, the slate average is easy: 8.5 out of 10.

<div style="margin: 2em 0 0.6em; padding: 0.7em 1.1em; background: linear-gradient(135deg, #2F241D 0%, #1c1c1c 100%); border-radius: 12px 12px 0 0;">
  <span style="font-size: 0.72em; font-weight: 800; letter-spacing: 0.16em; text-transform: uppercase; color: #C7A97B;">⚾ Jersey of the Day</span>
  <span style="display: block; font-size: 1.15em; font-weight: 900; color: #ffffff; margin-top: 3px;">Phillies Powder Blue</span>
</div>

<blockquote class="twitter-tweet" data-dnt="true"><a href="https://twitter.com/Phillies/status/2077835923216048149"></a></blockquote>

On a one-game night the pick makes itself: Philadelphia's powder blue. It is one of the best throwback looks in the National League, a soft sky-blue that reads beautifully under the lights at Citizens Bank Park, and it comfortably out-dressed the Mets' road grays across the diamond. Each day we spotlight one team's look straight from their own feed, so you see the jersey and uniform on the field and not just on the rack.

### Mets at Phillies

<div style="margin: 0 0 1.4em; padding: 0.65em 1em; background: rgba(155, 200, 36, 0.12); border-radius: 10px; border: 1px solid rgba(155, 200, 36, 0.25);">
  <p style="font-size: 1.05em; font-weight: 900; margin: 0; text-align: center; color: #9bc824;">Matchup Grade: 8.5 / 10</p>
</div>

The only game on the board, and a sharp one. The Mets opened the second half in their classic road grays with the blue-and-orange "New York" script, while the Phillies countered with the powder blue. Gray against powder blue is a clean, summery pairing, and Philadelphia's baby blue is the whole reason this one grades so high. The Phillies get the better uniform of the night, and it is not particularly close.

## Sunday, July 12

A 15-game getaway-day slate to close the weekend, and it played out the way Sundays usually do: powder blues and creams instead of Friday's City Connect parade. The Brewers and Blue Jays brought powder blue, the Rangers went powder too, the Astros wore orange, the Rockies purple, the Angels red, the Giants their signature cream, and the Padres broke out the camouflage. Slate average: 6.9 out of 10.

<div style="margin: 2em 0 0.6em; padding: 0.7em 1.1em; background: linear-gradient(135deg, #2F241D 0%, #1c1c1c 100%); border-radius: 12px 12px 0 0;">

Our pick from Sunday's slate: San Diego's camouflage jersey, the Padres' signature military tribute and one of the most recognizable alternate uniforms in baseball, worn at home against Toronto. Each day we spotlight one team's look straight from their own feed, so you see the uniform on the field and not just on the rack.

## Saturday, July 11

A big 16-game Saturday, headlined by the Brewers and Pirates finally playing Friday's rained-out game as part of a doubleheader in Pittsburgh. Saturday brought the color the midweek slates lacked: City Connects for the Giants and Blue Jays, powder blue for the Brewers and Royals, the Cardinals' cream, the Astros' orange, and a stack of alternates. Slate average: 7.0 out of 10.

## Friday, July 10

Fourteen games, with Brewers at Pirates rained out and rescheduled as a Saturday doubleheader, a washout that also claimed the Pirates' Friday-home black City Connect. The rest of the league more than covered for it: eight City Connects hit the field in one night after two straight nights of zero, exactly the weekend pattern this tracker was built to prove. Only two home teams stayed in white, seven road teams stayed in gray, and 19 of the 28 uniforms on the field were alternates or City Connects. Slate average: 7.5 out of 10, the best-dressed night of the log so far.

## Thursday, July 9

The full 13-game slate is in the books. A mostly classic Thursday: a dozen home teams in white or cream, six road teams in the standard grays, and eight clubs reaching for an alternate. Almost all the color came from the road, where seven visiting teams broke out an alternate, from the Brewers' powder blue and Mariners' navy to the Diamondbacks' black and the Cubs' and Royals' royal blues, while Cincinnati supplied the night's only home alternate in its red. No City Connects. Slate average: 6.9 out of 10.

## The Pattern Engine: Running Uniform Counts

This is where the tracker earns its keep over time. Every look gets counted, and as the days stack up this section becomes the evidence file behind every "what jersey are they wearing today" question: how often each team wears each uniform, and which days of the week each alternate actually shows up. 12 days in, 47 different alternates have been worn a combined 125 times. Here is the alternate board.

The day-of-week rhythm is now the clearest pattern in the data. Friday is City Connect night and it is not close: 15 of the 20 City Connects we have logged came on a Friday, with 4 more on Saturday and exactly one on any other day of the week. Weeknights are for the classics. Wednesday's 17-game slate produced 12 alternates and zero City Connects, which is the normal midweek shape, and the loud stuff waits for the weekend. The other reliable rhythm is the Sunday matinee, where the powder blues and creams come out: Brewers, Blue Jays, Rangers, Royals, Giants and Padres have all used that slot.

Two exceptions prove the rule. The Pirates' Friday-home black City Connect missed one week only because the game itself was rained out, and the Nationals were the lone club to skip their slot by choice, wearing the navy alternate instead of the District Blueprint. The single non-weekend City Connect on the board is Houston's white Space City set on a Monday. Friday is officially City Connect night in 2026, and the log now has the receipts.

## Frequently Asked Questions

**What is the MLB daily uniform tracker?**

It is a running log of what every MLB team actually wore in every game, updated each morning after the previous night's slate. Every jersey and uniform gets recorded, from home whites and road grays to alternates, City Connects, and throwbacks, and every matchup gets a ColorWay grade for how the two looks paired on the field.

**What jersey is my team wearing tonight?**

Open your team's page from the uniform schedule grid above. It maps every fixed jersey day the club runs, like Friday City Connects, Sunday creams, or powder blue day games, so you can call tonight's look before first pitch. Then check back here tomorrow morning, because this tracker logs the uniform every MLB team actually wore, every single day.

**How do I find out what jersey the Dodgers or Yankees are wearing tonight?**

The [Dodgers uniform schedule](/stories/dodgers-uniform-schedule-2026) and [Yankees uniform schedule](/stories/yankees-uniform-schedule-2026) pages predict tonight's jersey from each team's day-of-week rotation, and the daily log on this page records what they actually wore last night, from home whites to City Connects. The same goes for all 30 clubs through the team pages above, so the question has an answer both before and after every game.

**How do you know what uniform each team wore?**

We check every game the morning after using game photos and broadcast footage from the night before, then log both teams' uniforms. When something is ambiguous, like a City Connect versus a similar regular alternate, we verify against multiple dated game photos before recording it.

**How often do MLB teams wear their City Connect uniforms?**

Most City Connect uniforms follow a fixed weekly slot. The Pirates wear their all-black 2026 City Connect for Friday home games, the Reds wear their pinstriped City Connect 2.0 on Saturdays, and most other clubs use similar weekend anchors. The running counts on this page will show the real frequency as the season plays out.

**Do MLB teams always wear white at home and gray on the road?**

No, and that is exactly why this tracker exists. White at home and gray on the road is the default, but on the Friday, July 10 slate alone, 19 of the 28 uniforms on the field were alternates or City Connects. Some clubs, like the Phillies, follow a strict published rotation, while others swap in alternates whenever the mood or the starting pitcher calls for it.

**When do MLB teams wear their alternate jerseys?**

It varies by team. Some alternates have fixed days, like Friday reds or Sunday creams, while others are manager's choice or a starting pitcher's preference. That day-of-week data is exactly what this tracker is built to surface, and the pattern section above fills in with evidence as the days accumulate.

## The Bottom Line on the MLB Daily Uniform Tracker

Baseball puts on more uniforms than any other sport, and nobody logs what actually hits the field each night. That is the gap this page fills: every team, every game, every day, graded and counted, with the day-of-week patterns emerging as the record grows. Bookmark it, check it with your morning coffee, and when you want to know what your team wears next, the [full uniform schedule guide](/stories/mlb-uniform-schedule-2026) has the forward-looking answer.
